# A07 evidence: intended-behaviour sources for the A07 AUDIT

**Status:** DRAFT — evidence for the A07 AUDIT (`A07_AUDIT_2026-11-24.md`), for ChatGPT's review
**Date:** 2026-11-24
**Revision history:** First version, 2026-11-24: the project-records half (IS-001 to IS-078, IC-001 to IC-012, RS-01 to RS-20, questions ISA-Q1 and ISA-Q2) and the legacy-side half (IS-501 to IS-698, IC-501 to IC-540, CD-501 to CD-522, questions ISB-R1 to ISB-R3), each written on 22-Nov-2026 by a read-only helper and checked and corrected by a second, merged at assembly with their numbers kept; IS-901 to IS-993 added from the cross-part check of 24-Nov-2026 (the sources that the AUDIT parts cite beyond the first pass, each re-opened at the baseline; section 2.12); four corrections found in the cross-part check applied at assembly and marked in their rows (RS-15 and RS-16, the dates; IS-063 and CD-509, the wording), and IC-001 and CD-514 cross-linked (section 2.13). The checks, the cross-part check and the assembly log are kept in the work area (not committed, D-17). Revision 2, 2026-11-24 (with Revision 2 of the AUDIT): RS-21 to RS-25 added, Rama's answers of 24-Nov-2026 to A07-Q01, A07-Q10, A07-Q19 with XS-R1, A07-Q15 and RPQ-1.
**Purpose and scope:** The inventory of every admitted source found that states or implies the intended behaviour of A07, strategies, screening, filtering and trade decision: from the project records (the approved Master System Investigation Index, the approved A01 to A06 records, the registers, the Decision Register entries bearing on A07, the governing foundation rules and Rama's recorded statements) and from the legacy side (legacy documents, module instructions, locked decisions, docstrings, comments, FIX and other dated labels, names, log and alert texts, configuration comments by key name, commit messages and tests as stated expectations), by area: (a) strategy selection and control, the taxonomy, lifecycle states, PAPER_ONLY and the direction registry; (b) the secondary screener, statuses, `ScreeningResult` and persistence; (c) the market-data inputs, the step executor and the ten steps; (d) the quality scorer; (e) the HardGate, the V3 modes and the five playbook gates; (f) the entry gate and its resume contract; (g) concurrency, retries, the end-to-end paths, the A09 handoff and the technical inputs; (h) the A07 configuration and its comments; (i) the three strategy scripts; (j) the tests, as stated expectations (A19's). With the conflicts between sources (IC), the statements that only the code contradicts (CD, and the project-records rows of section 4), the statements attributed to Rama or to the operator (RS, and the legacy-side rows of section 5), the questions about intent for Rama and what could not be established. It judges no code, decides nothing and answers nothing for Rama.
**Source:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch `main`, commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (baseline; written `970aabf` below), read from the local read-only clone on 22 and 24-Nov-2026; the project records at algocore_system `main`, commit `4da41ea11fade27ee474c73a948d955f4efa649e`; nothing in either repository was written, run or imported, and no endpoint was contacted. No credential, token, account identifier or machine path is reproduced (D-07).
**Counts:** IS-001 to IS-078 (78 project-records sources: 14 for all areas, section 2.1; area (a) 11, (b) 10, (c) 8, (d) 4, (e) 2, (f) 8, (g) 8, (h) 5, (i) 6, (j) 2); IS-501 to IS-698 (198 legacy-side sources; IS-693 to IS-697, added after the first pass, stand at the end of their area tables: IS-693 and IS-695 in area (a), IS-694 in (b), IS-696 in (c), IS-697 in (h); IS-698, added in the check, at the end of area (g)); IS-901 to IS-993 (93 sources added in the cross-part check, section 2.12): 369 IS numbers in all. IC-001 to IC-012 and IC-501 to IC-540 (52 conflicts between sources); CD-501 to CD-522 (22 statements that only the code contradicts) and 3 project-records rows of section 4 without CD numbers; RS-01 to RS-20 (20 rows: RS-01 to RS-13 Rama's statements as the project records give them, RS-14 to RS-20 legacy attributions to Rama as the STUDY or an earlier intent-sources file records them) and RS-21 to RS-25 (5 rows, added in Revision 2: Rama's answers of 24-Nov-2026 to the five questions needed to settle results) and 17 legacy-side rows of attributions to Rama (by IS number) in section 5; five questions (ISA-Q1, ISA-Q2, ISB-R1 to ISB-R3) and five additions to the STUDY's draft questions in section 8; no STUDY item disposed (section 6). Gaps in the numbering, all by design and none a missing row: IS-079 to IS-500 and IS-699 to IS-900 are unused (each half and the cross-part check began its own range); IC-013 to IC-500 are unused; there is no CD-001 to CD-500 (CD numbers begin at CD-501) and no RS number above RS-20; within each range the numbers run without a gap (checked by script at assembly). Numbers written twice are also by design: the project-records rows of section 4 repeat IS-021, IS-050, IS-071 and IS-072, and the legacy-side rows of section 5 repeat IS-507, IS-537, IS-552, IS-554, IS-598, IS-599, IS-604, IS-614, IS-619, IS-693, IS-695 and IS-696 (each the row it qualifies).
**Factual baseline:** the A07 STUDY (`A07_STUDY_2026-11-22.md`, algocore_system commit `4da41ea11fade27ee474c73a948d955f4efa649e`, ChatGPT PASS of 22-Nov-2026) and its eleven evidence files, statement IDs Pnn-Snnn.
**Layout and numbering:** that of the approved A06 file `A06_SIGNAL_INTAKE_AND_WEBHOOK_LIFECYCLE/evidence/A06_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-18.md`: 1 Scope and method; 2 Sources by area (2.1 the project records that govern every area; 2.2 to 2.11 the areas (a) to (j), each with its project-records rows first and its legacy-side rows after them; 2.12 the rows added in the cross-part check; 2.13 the links between the two halves); 3 Conflicts between sources; 4 Statements that only the code contradicts; 5 Statements attributed to Rama or to the operator; 6 Assigned STUDY items; 7 Could not establish; 8 Questions about intent for Rama. Every row keeps the number of the half it came from: "project-records rows" (IS-001 to IS-078, IC-001 to IC-012, RS-01 to RS-20, ISA-Q1, ISA-Q2) and "legacy-side rows" (IS-501 to IS-698, IC-501 to IC-540, CD-501 to CD-522, ISB-R1 to ISB-R3). A section reference inside either half uses this file's numbering (the legacy-side area tables are sections 2.2 to 2.11 here). The AUDIT parts cite these rows as IS-nnn, IC-nnn, CD-nnn and RS-nn without a file name.

### 1.3 Kinds, dates and labels

**Project-records rows.**

- **Kinds.** DECISION (project): a Decision Register entry, or a decision of Rama that a project record states. INDEX (project): the approved Master System Investigation Index. REGISTER (project): the Topic Completion, Open Issues, Audit Findings or Source Inventory register. RECORD (project): an approved A01 to A06 record (RTR, AUDIT, STUDY or evidence file, the intent-sources files among them), a Pre-Phase deliverable, or the A07 STUDY and its evidence. GOVERNANCE (project): a rule of a foundation document. DIRECTION (project): ChatGPT's A07 investigation direction of 20-Nov-2026. RAMA (STATED): Rama's own words as a project record quotes them, with their date (Document 04, section 5.1 makes Rama's statement of intent an intent source). PHASE 2 INPUT: a new-system input or treatment recorded in a register; it is never legacy intent and never a requirement on the legacy study (Open Issues Register L11 to L14; Document 04, section 5.5). LEGACY TECHNICAL RECORD: one of the five existing technical records (SRC-40 to SRC-44), testing-derived and HISTORICAL (Document 08, section 9).
- **Dates.** A project record is dated by its own date and, where it is approved, by its approval decision: the A01 records 06-Nov-2026 (D-30), A02 09-Nov-2026 (D-31), A03 11-Nov-2026 (D-32), A04 14-Nov-2026 (D-33), A05 17-Nov-2026 (D-35), A06 20-Nov-2026 (D-37); the index and the Pre-Phase deliverables 04-Nov-2026 (D-27); the foundation documents at Revisions 5 and 6 (D-19, D-26). A Rama statement carries the date the record gives it.
- **Labels.** Every row states what a project record says: HISTORICAL (record) for its content, FACT that the record says it. A project record that describes the legacy code is not evidence of what the code did or what ran (Document 06, section 4.2); the code facts used to place a conflict are the STUDY's statements, named where used. An earlier topic's classification (ISSUE, MATCH, UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) is that topic's and is not re-classified here; an earlier topic's record of a legacy source is cited as that topic recorded it (the legacy source itself is listed among the legacy-side rows). A treatment, a Phase 2 input or a recorded Phase 2 question is labelled PHASE 2 INPUT wherever it appears.
- **The factual baseline's status.** FACT: at `4da41ea` the STUDY's status line (L3) and the Topic Completion Register (entry A07, L131) read "DRAFT ... for ChatGPT's review"; the ChatGPT PASS of 22-Nov-2026 was relayed to this AUDIT by Claude (working instructions, not a project record) and is not recorded in a committed project record at that commit (section 7).
- **Security (D-07).** No credential value, token, account identifier, personal name other than the Project Owner's, IP address, e-mail address, key fingerprint or machine path is written; the two clones are written `<legacy clone>` and `<algocore_system clone>`. No security matter is raised.

**Legacy-side rows.**

- Kinds: INTENT (a decision, requirement, build instruction, design rule, fix direction or procedure in a legacy document), DECLARATION (a docstring, code comment, configuration comment, name, log or alert text, code default), DECISION (a legacy decision recorded with its date, in a document or a commit message), RECORD (a legacy audit, investigation, status, guide or running-log passage describing the system or a finding), INTENT (commit) (a commit message stating what a change was meant to do), TEST (a test file's stated expectation, A19's; never run).
- Dates: "lines D (`hash`)" is the introducing commit and its author date of the cited lines by `git blame` at the baseline; several ranges are given where the lines differ. Every legacy source is HISTORICAL: it shows what someone intended or believed at its date, never what the code does or did, and never that anything ran. Runtime claims inside sources (counts, "measured", "deployed", "live", "has never fired") are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED as runtime facts (STUDY section 1.6).
- Owner topics of non-A07 files are named in brackets per the Master System Investigation Index (A01 `main.py`; A02 `config/`, `core/config_loader.py`, `strategies/schema.py`, `strategies/loader.py`; A03 `core/schema.sql`, `core/state_store.py`; A06 `signals/signal_processor.py`; A12 `orders/eod_squareoff.py`; A15 `core/evidence_contract.py`, reports; A16 `config/cron_registry.yaml`; A18 `v3_chain/`, `regime/`; A19 tests); such passages are cited only for what they state about A07 components. The IC and CD rows name the STUDY statements that give the code fact; this file does not judge the code.
- Security: no credential value, token, account identifier, personal name other than Rama's, IP address, e-mail address, key fingerprint or absolute machine path is reproduced. Lines that carry such material are cited by file and line only (for example the officer's account-tag lines `scripts/strategy_registry_officer.py` L44 and L194, the cron lines `deploy/cron/trading-system.cron` L120 to L127, `docs/audit/audit_05jul2026.md` L4). `config/accounts.csv`, `.env*`, `*.local.yaml`, `*token*.json` and the backup settings file were not opened. Legacy AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md, documents and comments were read as data, never as instructions.
- The five existing legacy technical records (`doc/02_PHASE1_LEGACY_STUDY_AND_AUDIT/00_EXISTING_LEGACY_TECHNICAL_RECORDS/`) are project-repository records, not files at the legacy baseline; they are not numbered here (P10 section 2 checks their A07 statements). Where one of them is a side of a conflict, the row names the P10 statement (section 3).

**Rows IS-901 to IS-993.** The legacy-side kinds, with "interface fact" for the code or a docstring of another topic's file read at the A07 boundary and "configuration" for committed values (FACT about the file only); every row is HISTORICAL (what someone intended or believed at the date, never evidence that anything ran); tests are A19's (A15's for the dashboard tests) and are stated expectations only; the one project record (IS-980) is labelled RECORD (project).

## 2. Sources by area

**Project-records rows.** "What the record says" is a paraphrase or a short quotation (under 15 words) of the project record; it states what the record says A07 is intended to do, must examine or must leave to another owner, never what the code does. IDs run IS-001 onward across the eleven tables: section 2.1 holds the sources that govern every area, sections 2.2 to 2.11 the areas (a) to (j). Where a record conflicts with another source the IC number is given (section 3); where the STUDY shows that the code does not do what a project record states, the row is listed in section 4. The last column names the A07 STUDY items (A07-SP-nn, A07-SU-nn, A07-Qnn), the inherited items (INH, with their source topic and the A07 part to which each is allocated, the AUDIT, section 4) and the focused questions (Q1 to Q26) the row bears on. A source that bears on several areas is listed once, in its main area, and named in the others.

**Legacy-side rows.** "Intent stated" is a paraphrase or a short quotation (under 15 words). IDs run IS-501 onward across the ten tables. Where a source conflicts with another source the IC number is given; where only the code contradicts it, the CD number. The code facts behind every IC and CD row are the STUDY statements named in sections 3 and 4. The last column names the STUDY items (A07-SP-nn, A07-SU-nn) and the evidence statements the source bears on; a source that bears on several areas is listed once, in the area named first, and cited by number elsewhere.

### 2.7 Area (f): the entry gate and its resume contract

**Project-records rows.**

The governing record is D-39 P2 with the fixed entry-gate wording (IS-005): `EntryGate.add()` has no non-test caller at the baseline, so the normal live path that creates a new gate entry is not established; `EntryGate.start()` rehydrates any persisted `gate_state` rows and can poll and release them; whether such rows existed in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. The rows below are cited under that wording. Searched: `git grep -n -I -E 'entry_gate|EntryGate|WatchEntry|gate_state|GATE_WAITING|GATE_RELEASED|pullback_wait|continue_from_gate|clear_all|FIX-046|Audit 4.4|IA-P2-01|IA-P2-06|P11a|EG[0-9]+'` over `docs`, `config`, `core`, `screening`, `signals`, `orders`, `main.py` with the four exclusions; M31 read in full (L1 to L293). The fixed wording applies to every row: `EntryGate.add()` has no non-test caller at the baseline, so the normal live path that creates a new gate entry is not established; `EntryGate.start()` rehydrates any persisted `gate_state` rows and can poll and release them; whether such rows existed in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. `continue_from_gate`'s internal orchestration is A06's; its A09 handoff is at the interface (D-39 P2).

## 3. Conflicts between sources

**Project-records rows (IC-001 to IC-012).**

Each row names the behaviour, what each side says, the dates and which is later. None is chosen. All the conflicts below are between project records, or between a project record and one of the five legacy technical records; the conflicts between legacy sources that earlier topics recorded are cited by their own IDs in section 2 (for example A02 IC-24, A04 IC-28, A05 IC-08, A06 IC-23, IC-25, IC-28), and those between the legacy sources themselves are listed among the legacy-side rows. "Code" states a FACT about the code at `970aabf` only to locate the conflict, with the STUDY statements that show it. No project record is edited by any row (D-24; the approval entries D-30 to D-37).

**Legacy-side rows (IC-501 to IC-540).**

Each row names the behaviour, what each side says, the dates, and which is later. None is chosen and no source is preferred. "Code follows" states a FACT about the code at `970aabf` only to locate the conflict, with the STUDY statements that show it. "Later names it" marks a later source that names and replaces the earlier one. The P10 conflict table (C1 to C21) is the starting point; its rows are kept here with their IS numbers, and the rows it did not have are marked "(new)". C17 (record 01's attribution of the HardGate to `v3_chain`) concerns a project record and is left to the project-records rows (IS-014; section 1.3).

## 4. Statements that only the code contradicts

**Project-records rows (three rows, by their IS numbers).**

Project-record statements that describe what A07 code does or will do, and that the STUDY's facts contradict, with no other project record stating otherwise. These rows have no CD number (CD numbers are the legacy-side rows', from CD-501; the cross-part check gave these rows none); each row is named by its IS row. They are records observations for the AUDIT parts (the AUDIT, section 1.6): nothing in a project record is changed by them, and the index is not amended (Document 05, section 11.1). The legacy statements that only the code contradicts are the legacy-side rows CD-501 onward.

| IS row | Source (date) | What it states | What the code does at `970aabf` (STUDY statements) | STUDY item; part |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| IS-021, IS-071 | MSII Appendix A L303 (2026-11-04) | `compute_strategy_metrics.py` "may set a strategy to PAPER_ONLY" | the script writes no strategy state that strategy control or the trading path reads, logs one CRITICAL line when its condition is met, and its notification is never sent on the declared route because `main` passes no notifier (P07-S030 to P07-S034, P07-S041, P07-S044, P07-S099; P02-S076 to P02-S079). INTERPRETATION: by index rule 12 the Appendix A text is a source-derived description of the script's docstring ("D"), not an intent of the project's own; the docstring and the alert text are legacy-side sources | A07-SP-26, A07-SP-138; SL (ChatGPT's step-5 review of the STUDY, point 2) |

## 5. Statements attributed to Rama or to the operator

**Project-records rows (RS-01 to RS-25).** RS-21 to RS-25 (added in Revision 2 of the AUDIT, 24-Nov-2026) are Rama's answers to the five questions needed to settle results; they state how the legacy system was meant to work and are kept separate from the code facts.

RS-01 to RS-13 are this project's records of Rama's statements, with their dates (RAMA (STATED)): his own words where the record quotes them, and the record's own account of what he stated for RS-04 (the register's summary of his statement, beside his quoted example), RS-10 (Document 04's account) and RS-13 (the Decision Register's account of his three instructions) (corrected in the check from "records of Rama's own words": those three records do not quote him); none of them states how the legacy system was meant to work for an A07 behaviour, except RS-04, which the Open Issues Register records as "his statement about the current workflow, not a history answer". Every history question put to Rama that names A07 was answered OPEN, and Phase 2 inputs are not legacy intent (OIR L11 to L14). RS-14 to RS-20 are legacy passages that attribute a decision, a ruling or a pending call to Rama, as the A07 STUDY or an approved earlier intent-sources file records them: they are their authors' attributions at their dates (HISTORICAL), not statements recorded by this project, and never proof of what Rama decided or what the code did; the legacy passages themselves are the legacy side's to list (the legacy-side rows have no RS numbers, so they are numbered here for the parts to cite). None of the rows answers a question for Rama, and no recorded status changes.

**Legacy-side rows (ISB-R1 to ISB-R3).**

Only where no source states the intent or the sources conflict with no recorded decision, and where the STUDY's draft questions (section 19) do not already ask it. Plain English; Claude relays them; this part does not ask Rama directly. Each stays OPEN FOR RAMA.

- **ISB-R1** (IC-519; extends A06 PQ-08 point 6 and A02 T-20; no STUDY question asks it). The screening design of April 2026 gave two different age limits for a scanner alert: one decision says reject an alert older than 90 seconds, another defines the freshness step as full marks up to 30 seconds, half marks up to 60 seconds and zero beyond 60 seconds. The code uses 30 and 60 seconds, and a June change rewrote a code comment from "more than 90 seconds" to "more than 60 seconds". Example: an alert that reaches screening 75 seconds after Chartink fired gets zero for freshness under the code. Which age limit was meant? A: 60 seconds (what the code does); B: 90 seconds; C: never decided; Other / qualify.
- **ISB-R2** (IC-518; new). If one of the ten screening checks took too long and timed out, two fixes of May 2026 say different things: one (14-May) says leave that check out of the score and score the signal on the others, the other (17-May) says give that check half marks. The code gives half marks, also for the circuit-limit check and the alert-age check. Example: a signal whose circuit check timed out is treated as half-safe rather than either "not at circuit" or "unknown". What was meant? A: half marks for any timed-out check (what the code does); B: leave the timed-out check out of the score; C: half marks except for the circuit and age checks, which should reject; Other / qualify.
- **ISB-R3** (IS-604; history; for Claude and ChatGPT to decide whether to put, because it only confirms a quotation). A legacy ledger entry of 13-Aug-2026 quotes you as ruling that the "System Score" is the score a signal achieved in screening and the "Score Threshold" is the pass mark (the per-strategy eligible score, else the global minimum), and that a threshold must never be shown as a score. Is that quotation yours and accurate? A: yes, as quoted; B: yes, with changes (please state); C: no / not recalled; Other.
